# Reconstruction evidence ledger

Updated: 2026-10-09. This ledger tracks the entire [implementation plan](04_implementation_plan.md); an empty evidence cell is incomplete, not a passed gate.

## Unlinked private continuation foundation (2026-10-07)

The new private handoff ledger and server service create one encrypted, fifteen-minute setup continuation from a fresh signed unlinked receipt. Recipient, private space and receiver are fixed; preparation also completes the ingress job and publication. Per-sender limits, changed-receiver/current-link checks and expiry prevent obsolete issuance. The fixed reply carries a fragment token, never incoming text or host data. Delivery uses stable IDs, current authorization, fenced leases and reconciliation without resend. The resolver exposes only server-side route metadata and grants no host authority.

The complete 35-migration disposable rebuild, 930 SQL assertions across 17 suites, 13 Auth/database integrations, 70 app/provider plus four SMTP tests, separate builds, built-server smoke and actual eve crash recovery pass. Local advisors report no issues. The final focused integration additionally verifies receiver disablement during provider preflight. UI/browser behavior is unchanged, so the complete browser journey is covered by the preceding slice and green CI for `22a2c35`. Generated SQL preserves prior history and repeats only the existing unchanged booking-event constraint alongside the handoff changes.

The preceding reply code/evidence CI runs failed on an early 204 stream response before session binding. Commit `22a2c35` waits for that legitimate asynchronous boundary; both full CI runs `37567549976` and `37567545550` passed. The crash/recovery assertions remain unchanged.

This is a foundation within task 4.2, not completed iMessage-first onboarding. There is no automatic handoff endpoint or cron wiring yet. Browser exchange, browser proof binding, authenticated admission and fresh private OTP verification remain next, followed by controlled live acceptance. Host setup remains 9/20 and all complete phase/AC gates remain open. Code `ee9ca0b` is deployed as `dpl_5MgFhKK79TPSPs2oAmTvDdJ1eioG`, verified Ready and aliased to `https://release.findmeatime.com`. The intended Vercel project and active FindMeATime2 (`mriseqztcwmezvtawnbo`, us-west-1) were reidentified before the single reviewed migration push. All 35 migrations are current; remote advisors report no issues. A rollback-only SQL probe passed handoff deduplication, private token checks, stale-lease fencing, no-resend recovery and receiver-disable suppression. Follow-up counts confirm zero receivers, inboxes, links and handoffs, zero automatic handoff jobs, service-only RPC execution and no direct table read.

Upload preflight checked 321 regular files against 12 configured credentials with no matches. Public pages and web/eve health return 200, anonymous workers return 401 and the unconfigured receiver returns 503. `releaseReady` remains false. No external message or browser handoff was performed. Code CI runs `37568485830` and `37568481680` were still in progress at evidence capture.

## Durable private iMessage replies (2026-10-07)

The real eve channel now captures final assistant text in its checkpoint and commits one frozen private reply together with input settlement. Repeated completion cannot replace it. Failed/empty output offers browser recovery, and overlong output links to the full web history. Web inputs create no iMessage reply. Service-only claims freeze the exact inbound route, persist uncertainty before sending and recheck current authority after preflight. Expired leases and lost acknowledgments reconcile the same provider reference without another send; unknown references remain uncertain. Revocation suppresses work without fabricating a provider result, and terminal/revoked intents clear private text. The dedicated reply cron/Next.js route reuse current Vault credentials and the pinned Photon transport.

The complete 34-migration disposable local rebuild passes. All 886 assertions across 16 SQL suites, 12 real Auth/database integrations, 70 app/provider and four SMTP tests pass. Separate eve/web builds, built-server smoke (including anonymous reply-worker denial), actual runtime process-kill recovery and the complete browser journey pass. Local security advisors report no issues; all 12 OpenSpec items pass strict validation. The SQL suites and committed integration fixtures run sequentially to avoid contaminating global SQL fixture assertions. The generated migration repeats the unchanged booking-event constraint; applied history is preserved.

A production-channel eve fixture forces settlement to fail before SQL commit, waits for completed output, kills/restarts the runtime and recovers the same saved reply without another mock-model call. Concurrent reply workers lose a committed result acknowledgment and reconcile once without resending. A separate private reply is paused during provider preflight; unlink then prevents sending and removes its body. Other assertions retain one private draft, canonical web continuation, no iMessage reply for web-only input and no policy/booking effect. These are local synthetic transport fixtures, not live device delivery. Task 4.4 is complete for this implemented recovery contract; the setup change is 9/20, with inbound-first linking, complete cross-channel setup and controlled live acceptance still open. All phase/release acceptance gates remain open.

The four preceding linked-input code/evidence CI runs (`37565130313`, `37565127269`, `37565536215`, `37565532240`) completed successfully. Code `195c03b` is deployed as `dpl_9981LQ1SSn7sHck1UGpj4eTEVMxw`, verified Ready at `https://release.findmeatime.com`. The selected FindMeATime2 project (`mriseqztcwmezvtawnbo`, active, us-west-1) and Vercel project `prj_eCihziUF85AHPkfnFCNhBtdYlfnk` were reidentified before the single-migration dry run/push and deployment. All 34 migrations are current; remote advisors report no issues. A rollback-only production SQL probe verified frozen reply creation, deduplication, crash reconciliation, stale-lease rejection and unlink suppression. Follow-up counts confirm zero receiver, inbox, link, channel-grant and reply records; the reply RPC is service-only, direct reply reads are denied and its named recovery job is installed. No provider messages were sent.

Upload preflight checked 316 regular files against 12 configured credentials with no matches. Public pages and web/eve health return 200, anonymous reply/code workers return 401 with `no-store`, and `releaseReady` remains false. The authenticated reply-worker probe returns redacted `CONFIGURATION_UNAVAILABLE`/503 because production still has no Photon project credentials; the receiver is likewise inactive. This verifies failure behavior, not a configured production send or scheduled reply. Code CI runs `37567145760` and `37567140749` passed their repository jobs but exposed a test timing assumption: the crash fixture requested a stream before canonical-session binding and received the expected 204. The fixture now waits for a bound session before inspecting completion; this does not change the production route or weaken the crash/reply assertions. Fresh CI is required for the correction.

## Photon transport foundation (2026-10-07)

The installed eve 0.71.3 Photon adapter was exercised with synthetic signed messages and an isolated downstream promise. It verifies signatures but returns HTTP 200 before that promise completes; replay calls the downstream processor again. Its `postMessage` path supplies no application-owned send identity. This selects an application-owned receiver/outbox boundary within the existing service graph. It does not establish live Spectrum send/reconciliation compatibility or justify a separate bridge process.

`POST /api/providers/photon` now verifies bounded raw bytes, timestamp, receiver identity and consistent private routing, then awaits a service-only database receipt. New private receiver/inbox tables provide project/message deduplication, immutable routing/content, receipt ordering and atomic inbox/job creation. The registry defaults disabled; no inbox creates host, link, execution or booking authority. The additional migration preserves all prior history; pg-delta also repeats the unchanged booking event-ID constraint, as in earlier migrations.

Local verification: `npm run check` passes 61 application/provider tests and 4 SMTP tests; separate eve/web builds and built-runtime smoke pass, including unsigned Photon denial. The full disposable migration reset and 13 pgTAP suites pass 738 assertions. Ten integration tests pass, including eight concurrent signed retries, a lost database response after commit and observed lock contention against receiver disablement. Local advisors report no findings; all 12 OpenSpec items pass strict validation. UI/browser code was unchanged in this slice, so the existing browser suite was not rerun locally. All four prior extracted-answer code/evidence CI runs completed successfully.

Read-only Spectrum inspection returned HTTP 200 and zero registered webhooks for the configured project. This does not establish absence of independent streaming consumers, target eligibility or delivery. No webhook was registered and no external message was sent. Linking, private-authority dispatch, outbound recovery and live acceptance remain pending; setup task 4.3 and all phase/release gates remain open.

Code commit `0ea1040` is deployed as `dpl_FsQ34cBLb1v8FTMuDkzLvohzr1nj` at `https://release.findmeatime.com` (Vercel `READY`). After reviewing the single pending migration, applied it to verified project `mriseqztcwmezvtawnbo`; all 31 migrations are current and remote advisors report no issues. The RPC denies anonymous/authenticated execution and allows only service execution. A rollback-only remote probe passed duplicate/conflict and receiver-disable checks; follow-up counts confirm zero receiver, inbox and Photon job records. Web/eve health return 200 with release readiness still false. The unconfigured Photon endpoint returns a redacted 503 with `no-store`, so production has no active receiver. Upload preflight checked 285 regular files against 12 configured secrets with no matches. Code CI runs `37559224671` and `37559221180` are still in progress at this record.

## Browser-bound iMessage linking backend (2026-10-07)

The backend now persists one browser/session/recipient-bound challenge as its own durable send intent, with encrypted temporary code material and a keyed proof hash. Protected typed endpoints expose masked state, immutable start/guess replay, explicit cancel/skip/unlink and one active owner per host/recipient. Codes expire after ten minutes; five guesses, one-minute cooldowns and per-host/per-recipient hourly caps are enforced in PostgreSQL. A separate HttpOnly binding request precedes code creation. No phone/code input is routed through the model or transcript.

The scheduled worker records uncertainty before external dispatch, fixes the line and client message ID, reauthorizes after provider preflight, and fences completion by lease. A lost acknowledgment or expired lease leads only to read-only reconciliation; a missing provider reference does not justify another send. Unexpected database errors abort rather than revoke challenges. The pinned low-level Photon SDK supports the application-owned client ID absent from the eve wrapper. A real token-issuance probe confirmed the configured shared route without looking up a recipient or sending a message.

Local evidence: all 32 migrations rebuild from scratch; 14 SQL suites pass 799 assertions, including 61 new linking assertions. Eleven real-Auth/database integrations pass, including concurrent start/worker races, lost provider and verification responses, competing number ownership, stale unlink, observed lock waits and logout during preflight. `npm run check` passes 66 application/provider tests and four SMTP tests; separate eve/web builds and production-server smoke pass. Browser-helper tests verify the protected proof cookie and code-free responses; no inline UI is implemented in this slice, so complete browser/iMessage acceptance is still pending. Local advisors report no findings and all twelve OpenSpec items pass strict validation.

The receiver registry remains disabled/unconfigured in production. Inline cards, inbound-first linking, linked sender-to-host execution, general conversational outbound delivery, actual recipient eligibility/delivery and full Phase 7 remain incomplete. This advances tasks 3.3/4.1/4.4 without completing their full scope; the change stays 6/20 and no release gate is marked complete. All four preceding Photon ingress code/evidence CI runs (`37559224671`, `37559221180`, `37559415925`, `37559410216`) completed successfully.

Deployed code `75d5553` to `dpl_6Miy4VGHiQiFTPXasdzWmgEtxmB5` (`https://findmeatime-release-j6mwtc48y-justdodos-projects.vercel.app`), verified Ready at `https://release.findmeatime.com`. The intended Vercel project and active Supabase project `mriseqztcwmezvtawnbo` were reidentified before applying the single reviewed migration; all 32 migrations are current and remote advisors report no findings. A rollback-only production probe verified deduplicated intent creation, browser binding, uncertainty/reconciliation, single-use linking and no resurrection after unlink. After rollback: zero receivers, challenges and links; one active recovery schedule; anonymous/browser RPC execution denied and service execution allowed. Public probes confirmed web/eve health, anonymous linking/worker denial, cross-origin denial and the unconfigured receiver's redacted 503. No webhook, recipient lookup, external message or Calendar event was created. Upload preflight checked 298 regular files against 12 configured credential values with zero matches. Code CI runs `37561291841` and `37561286732` remain in progress at evidence capture; release readiness remains false.

## Inline iMessage linking and selected theme (2026-10-07)

The `/app` setup conversation now offers Connect/Maybe later after exact settings confirmation, then inline private-number and six-digit forms, masked delivery/expiry state and a server-verified connected summary. Change-number cancels the previous challenge, new-code requests re-enter the number, skip persists and unlink requires explicit confirmation. Lost start/verification responses retain immutable retry identity; raw codes clear on submit and reload. Visible status polling and focus refresh are fenced against stale reads during mutations. Disabled provider configuration keeps web continuation available. No phone/code action goes through eve, transcript or browser storage.

The selected shadcn `b6rtA2Hmi` theme/font were applied through the CLI while retaining the existing chat/AI Elements composition and eve transport. Next.js self-hosts Inter; olive/green semantic tokens now also style the landing/Auth shell. Visual review found the floating latest-message control overlapping inline actions, so it now occupies a separate footer with a 44px target. Wrong-code recovery returns focus to the empty invalid input. Measured muted-text contrast was initially 4.25:1 on white; reducing that token's OKLCH lightness to 0.54 meets 4.5:1 on both white and muted surfaces. Browser assertions cover those text pairs, primary actions, errors, code-slot height and non-overlapping scroll controls.

Local verification uses Chromium 153.0.8010.12 through Playwright 1.63.0, real disposable Supabase Auth/database state and isolated Google/Spectrum fixtures. The journey includes lost start and verification acknowledgments, same-identity retry, wrong-browser denial, wrong-code attempts, reload clearing, one link, unlink cancellation/confirmation, change-number revocation, expiry, known delivery failure and skip/resume. Private setup/runtime rows and browser storage are checked for code leakage. No live recipient lookup or message occurs. The final complete browser journey passes, together with 66 application/provider tests, four SMTP tests, types/documentation checks, separate eve/web builds and the built-runtime security smoke. No SQL changed; the preceding slice’s 32-migration rebuild, 799 database assertions and 11 real-Auth integrations remain the database baseline. All four previous linking code/evidence CI runs completed successfully.

Final visual verdict: pass for phone/code/action readability at 320px, desktop 1280px and 200% CSS magnification, reduced motion, visible keyboard focus, non-overlapping controls and no horizontal overflow. Actual iPhone Safari/native zoom and live private delivery remain unverified. Setup tasks 3.1 and 3.3 are complete, bringing the change to 8/20. Full cross-channel setup, public-link readiness, complete AC-28 and every phase/release exit remain open. The receiver remains inactive.

## Linked private setup execution (2026-10-07)

Signed receipts now freeze current link/receiver authority and transfer once to the canonical host setup conversation through an atomic service-only processor. Existing runtime recovery handles execution; each tool rechecks the phone link and current host authority. The processor respects active transport leases, recovers expired ones, preserves per-route receipt order and lets another host proceed while one conversation is busy. Web continuation shares the same eve session. Model changes remain private drafts with iMessage attribution; confirmation and booking authority are unchanged.

Local evidence: all 33 migrations rebuilt with Supabase 2.119.0; 15 SQL suites pass 846 assertions, including 47 linked-execution assertions. All 12 real-Auth/database integrations pass, including six duplicate signed deliveries, concurrent dispatch after a lost committed response, actual eve draft execution, web continuation in the same session and unlink winning an observed lock wait. Browser logout remains independent from phone unlink; account, admission, receiver, expiry and relink negatives pass. `npm run check` passes 67 application/provider and four SMTP tests; separate eve/web production builds, built-runtime security smoke and the actual-eve restart/replay conversation test pass. Local security advisors and database lint report no findings. The pg-delta migration adds receipt bindings/indexes and narrow functions/schedule, updates existing authorization/ingress/attribution, and repeats the unchanged booking event-ID check as earlier generated migrations do. No applied migration was edited.

The last inline-linking code/evidence CI runs (`37563438371`, `37563434087`, `37563660835`, `37563657328`) all completed successfully. No UI behavior changed in this slice, so the full browser visual journey was not rerun locally. Full onboarding, inbound-first continuation, conversational outbound reconciliation and live iMessage acceptance remain open. The setup change remains 8/20; the production receiver must remain inactive and release readiness remains false.

Internal dispatch is denied to anonymous/authenticated roles, allowed to service execution, and its recovery schedule is active. Post-rollback counts are zero for receivers, inbox receipts, links and channel grants. No webhook or live message was created.

## Change ownership

| Owner | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| rebuild-application-foundation | Phase 0 ledger, shared contracts/authorization, admission/request rebuild, protected route shells and CI |
| validate-provider-and-agent-compatibility | Phase 1 runtime/model/topology decisions and all live provider/client evidence |
| connect-google-calendars | Host/requester Calendar, separate optional guest identity, timezone and callback binding |
| conversational-host-setup | Draft/review, suggestions, calendar analysis and OTP setup linking |
| evaluate-calendar-and-travel-feasibility | Candidate filtering, both travel legs, manual allowance and private exceptions |
| book-approved-proposals-reliably | Approval, reservations, dispatch/reconciliation, receipt and transactional delivery |
| email continuity (change to create before implementation) | Signed AgentMail ingestion and same-request requester conversation |
| iMessage continuity (change to create before implementation) | Linked host proposal review and decisions beyond setup |
| agent access (change to create before implementation) | Public skills, MCP OAuth, CLI, scoped grants and discovery |
| release hardening (change to create before implementation) | Operational limits/runbooks, final deployment and full completion audit |

## Runtime foundation evidence

Verified locally on 2026-10-07, Node 24.21.0/npm 11.19.0:

- Pinned eve 0.71.3, Next.js 16.4.0, React 19.2.8, AI SDK 7.0.105 and Zod 4.4.3. Separate `npm run build:eve` and `npm run build:web` pass; root `vercel.ts` follows the peer-service template. The initial deployed service composition passes HTTPS health and anonymous-denial checks; see the deployment record below.
- `npm ci`, `npm run check`, `npm run build` and `npm run test:runtime` pass. The smoke test starts the production outputs, checks web health/security headers and verifies anonymous denial at all seven eve session/stream endpoints. CI now executes these commands without provider credentials. Deny-all is temporary; actor/session ownership and recovery tests remain outstanding.
- Supabase CLI 2.119.0: all six retained pgTAP suites pass, 424 assertions. This proves the retained baseline on the local stack, not rebuilt application acceptance.
- `npm run verify:model` calls direct OpenAI through `eve/models/openai`, using native ID `gpt-6-luna`, low reasoning, a synthetic prompt and a required structured tool call. The call passed (56 input / 18 output tokens), with no scheduling tool execution. The selected model has a documented 1,050,000-token context window; metadata is explicit to avoid dependence on AI Gateway discovery. See [official model documentation](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-6-luna).
- Missing/unapproved model configuration fails closed. Runtime refusal, quota/rate-limit, invalid-output and interrupted-turn behavior still need application-level verification. Only the current model is accepted until another is explicitly verified.
- A scan of 215 generated eve/web output files found none of the 15 configured secret values. Source-level client/server import enforcement remains to be added.
- Intended production persistence is managed Vercel Workflow; application authorization, grants, scheduling state and durable effects remain Supabase-owned. Shared memory and default agent tools are disabled. No extra worker/bridge is justified yet; restart/repeated-tool and Photon compatibility tests must settle that decision.

These results complete the initial build slice only. No user journey or release acceptance case is certified by them.

## Authorized conversation ingress and replay (local)

Verified on 2026-10-07: the tenth migration adds a service-only accepted-message inbox and canonical runtime binding. Custom conversation routes authenticate before open/read/send/stream, deny cross-request access, freeze input under a client UUID, serialize pending messages and reject silent replacement of a bound workflow. Default eve session/control routes remain denied. The inbox is temporarily capped at 200 messages per conversation, and text is bounded at 10,000 characters. Streams expose only text and safe lifecycle events, with per-event/idle authorization checks and cursor reconnect.

The full local reset and seven pgTAP suites pass 513 assertions. Eighteen application unit tests, four SMTP tests and four Auth/database integration tests pass. An actual eve 0.71.3 fixture uses the production channel and a deterministic mock model: a real details command commits, the process group is killed before the tool returns, `eve dev --resume` restarts the same workflow and a same-client-ID retry completes with no second revision. The model generates a different tool-call ID on replay; conversation/message/operation retry identity preserves the effect. A second message and cursor reconnect preserve continuity without replaying the first reply. Cross-request and revoked stream access are denied. `npm run test:conversations` runs this fixture in CI after local database setup, with no live model/provider calls.

The first crash-test attempts timed out after 30 seconds. Inspection of the pinned bundled Workflow runtime identified its default 860-second inline ownership lease. The isolated fixture sets `WORKFLOW_INLINE_OWNERSHIP_LEASE_SECONDS=5`; the resulting process-kill/replay test passes in about ten seconds. No production setting or framework file was changed. This establishes local replay after lease expiry, not the production recovery SLA.

Automatic recovery between database acceptance and runtime dispatch, managed Vercel recovery, complete host/private/shared runtime switching, browser credential exchange, remaining runtime controls and full product journeys are still pending. No foundation task or phase exit is marked complete by this slice.

## Accepted-message dispatch recovery

Local verification on 2026-10-07: two additional migrations add leased dispatch metadata and a minute Supabase Cron wake-up. Recovery scans the authoritative inbox, so it covers a crash between acceptance and runtime send without needing a queue publication. Claims use `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, five rows per sweep and 90-second leases. Completion uses the lease token; an expired worker cannot acknowledge a new claim. Transport acknowledgment leaves the turn pending, with a five-minute retry delay until the runtime receipt settles. Current-grant denial retires pending input without starting model work. Canonical runtime identity still prevents silent replacement.

`npm run test:conversations` now simulates accepted input without any send and verifies recovery through the authenticated endpoint, preserving the second-message stream and cursor. The complete local reset passes 523 database assertions; 20 app and four SMTP tests pass. The schedule is inert until its two environment-specific Vault entries exist. These checks do not certify managed Workflow crash latency, the remaining session controls or full product journeys.

## Browser conversation transport

Verified locally on 2026-10-07: admitted `/app` hosts and active `/booking/[bookingId]` requesters can send, stream and replay their authorized conversation through a cookie-authenticated web gateway. The gateway allowlists application routes, rejects arbitrary upstreams/framework controls, forwards current server-verified credentials and removes runtime-only fields. Absolute cursors deduplicate replay; completed text replaces partial blocks; retry after a lost acknowledgment uses the same message ID. Revocation aborts the reader and clears rendered history; closed requester access returns to the permitted receipt. Shared messages use a neutral participant label rather than claiming sender attribution.

## Explicit withdrawal and decline — 2026-10-07

The request-lifecycle adapter requires current host/guest authority, explicit confirmation, expected revision and a stable decision UUID. The service-only closure record is immutable; exact retries recover only minimal terminal status. Closure clears active proposal/evaluation pointers and revokes requester mutation/Calendar authority. Dispatch also rechecks request/time/evaluation freshness after locks. Job completion/failure checks use wall-clock expiry, and newly claimed leases begin at the actual claim time. ## Cloudflare transactional transport — 2026-10-07

The server adapter accepts a frozen account and message, permits only `no-reply@findmeatime.com`, and posts once to the selected Cloudflare account. A successful HTTP response alone is insufficient: the response must contain a message identity and affirmative queued/delivered evidence for the intended recipient. ## Fenced booking confirmation delivery — 2026-10-07

## Operator recovery and approval continuity — 2026-10-08

## Interruptible browser conversation recovery — 2026-10-08

The browser previously supplied only a page-lifetime abort signal, and Reconnect now woke only a retry delay. A stalled fetch could keep waiting despite that action. JSON transport now bounds the whole response, including body parsing, to 35 seconds; stream transport is bounded to 60 seconds, beyond the gateway's 55-second stream limit. A separate per-read controller lets manual reconnect interrupt current reads without resetting the transcript cursor or the independent frozen message input. Timeout never proves that a message was rejected; the same client UUID is retained for retry. Navigation and denied authority still abort the whole lifetime.

Real HTTP fixtures stall before headers and during JSON bodies, verify cancellation and recover an accepted request with the same input. The browser fixture first fails a snapshot, holds the next snapshot open, clicks Reconnect now and verifies an enabled composer without a workspace reload. Existing same-message acknowledgement-loss and revoked-access journeys pass. Inspected the recovered workspace screenshot. All 153 application/provider tests plus four SMTP tests, type/docs/script checks, both builds, browser journey, real-eve restart recovery and runtime smoke pass. ## AgentMail authenticated transport boundary — 2026-10-08

The internal `verifiedAgentMailReceipt` adapter and seven independent HMAC fixture tests establish the current AgentMail/Svix contract. Svix 2.7.0 verifies signatures and five-minute timestamp tolerance but returns no parsed payload; JSON parsing therefore follows successful verification. Raw UTF-8 is preserved, invalid encoding is rejected, headers and payload size are bounded, and a five-second read deadline cancels stalled streams. Only normal received-message events produce minimized event/delivery/inbox/thread/message locators and a SHA-256 signed-body digest. Configured inbox and duplicated thread context must agree. HTML, attachments, arbitrary sender/authentication headers and message text never become identity or model input here. Omitted bodies remain eligible for a later exact-message lookup.

Fixture evidence covers forged/old/future/swapped/changed-body deliveries, rotation signatures, unknown/blocked/spam/unauthenticated events, malformed input, conflicting inbox/thread, omitted content, stable retry evidence, changed-payload digests, stream cancellation and caller abort. This does not provide durable deduplication or sender/request authority. No HTTP receiver, provider registration, inbox routing, send or live webhook delivery is introduced; those remain Phase 6 obligations.

## Independent AgentMail author evidence — 2026-10-08

Code `715b72b` pins mailauth 7.1.1 and adds bounded raw-message retrieval plus independent DKIM verification. The provider's normal received label can coexist with a DMARC failure under policy `none`, so it cannot by itself establish sender alignment. The verifier requires exact From-domain alignment, signed From and Message-ID, complete body coverage, strict modern signatures and non-testing keys. It returns minimized domain evidence without granting request authority. See [contract and sources](03_provider_setup.md#independent-email-author-evidence).

Eight deterministic tests use independent RSA signatures and cover forged verdicts, altered body/identity, limited-body signatures, ambiguous From, testing keys, raw-download origin/credential isolation, URL expiry, size and stalled DNS/downloads. A controlled read-only provider probe downloaded 5,224 bytes and found matching From/Message-ID plus aligned full-body DKIM signatures. The signatures did not cover Message-ID, so author verification correctly rejected this Cloudflare message. This is negative live evidence; positive live requester-author acceptance remains unverified.

## Requester recovery authority foundation — 2026-10-08

Code `099ea03` adds private recovery records and service-only issuance/redemption. # Local OAuth resource isolation spike — 2026-10-08

Executed the retained `npm run probe:oauth` on Supabase CLI 2.119.0 and GoTrue v2.197.0, source commit `4eee58f296d9698a1c2c0ae14d7a0b379c7622d3`. | Probe | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Authorization-server discovery | S256 and plain advertised; code and refresh grants advertised |
| Dynamic public-client registration | 201; fixed loopback callback |
| Consent API | Exact client/callback/state round trip |
| Wrong JSON code-exchange resource | 400 |
| Wrong S256 verifier | 400 |
| Wrong form code-exchange resource | **200 — isolation failure** |
| Access token audience | **authenticated — not the requested MCP resource** |
| Authorization code reuse | 400 |
| Wrong-resource JSON refresh | **200 — isolation failure** |
| Refreshed token audience | **authenticated — not the requested MCP resource** |
| Refresh after grant revocation | 400 |

The retained probe prints `resourceIsolationPassed: false` and exits 2. Pinned source review confirms the form parser omits Resource and refresh delegates without a resource constraint. Its assertion that this version returns 200 documents the observed negative compatibility result; it is not acceptance of that behavior for the application. Do not infer hosted-version behavior or complete client compatibility from this local process.

Decision: reject stock Supabase OAuth as an unmodified protected-MCP boundary. Keep Supabase Google-only identity, but require explicit code/refresh resource binding, resource-specific tokens, current application grants, and protected-operation checks in the owning agent-access implementation. No MCP endpoint was exposed or authorization weakened. Compatibility task 2.1a is complete; full 2.1, requester grants, browser/terminal and all seven named-client gates remain open.

Both exploratory and retained runs cleaned up their temporary processes and synthetic users. A separate local database check found zero matching users and two soft-deleted client tombstones, with no active probe clients. The main local stack remained running. This increment adds a local probe and decision evidence only; no application runtime, schema, hosted configuration or deployment changed. Production remains at the verified quotation-readback deployment.

# Agent OAuth protocol and token primitives — 2026-10-08

Created the bounded `authorize-agent-clients` change and completed task 1.1. The first internal slice fixes issuer/resource from application configuration, validates exact callback syntax without silently normalizing registered values, requires S256, and parses raw form/query fields with duplicate-decoding, malformed-encoding and size/count defenses. Unknown extension fields grant no permissions. Host and requester read/write/decision scopes are distinct and cannot be mixed into one actor grant.

Pinned JOSE 6.2.12 supplies ES256 signing and verification. Access tokens contain only issuer, exact singleton MCP audience, actor/client/grant identifiers, canonical scopes, unique ID and bounded times. Expiry is the earlier of five minutes or grant expiry. Issuance rechecks current authority before and after signing; verification checks it after signature/claim validation and rechecks wall-clock expiry afterward. Returned claims do not pass the existing application-credential guard. The next durable-authority task must provide real callbacks before any endpoint uses these primitives.

Key configuration rejects missing/malformed keys, duplicate IDs, mismatched private/public components and private material in retiring keys. An active key and at most three retiring public keys support rotation; public projection excludes private fields. Token-supplied remote/embedded keys are rejected. Configuration examples contain no keys, and no signing material is provisioned in production by this slice.

# Durable agent OAuth grants — 2026-10-08

Authorization task 1.2 now includes private grants, hashed one-minute codes, rotating refresh credentials, service-only consent/check/revoke RPCs and current underlying authority. Explicit same-browser decisions are immutable; exact grant/code retries recover the recorded decision without issuing another code. Host consent verifies a fresh browser JWT, delegates against the original current Auth session and admission for at most thirty days, and is bounded by the session maximum. Requester grants are bounded by the current request/token lifetime. Observed authority loss permanently revokes the grant. No login, continuation or provider token is returned or stored in grant projections.

Code consumption is atomic across concurrent callers and binds client/resource/callback/S256. Refresh preserves or narrows permissions, consumes exactly one credential and retains spent hashes. A reused consumed credential revokes its entire family, including a concurrent winner; later signing/operation adapters must recheck current authority and preserve committed error outcomes. Narrowing a grant rejects earlier broader tokens, and check projections preserve narrower verified-token scopes. Browser and refresh-credential revocation are implemented without meeting agreement or approval effects. Public protocol routes, browser consent/revocation UI, client-operation integration and controlled live authorization remain unfinished under tasks 2.1–3.1.

# Bounded runtime history reader — 2026-10-09

The internal `readRuntimeHistory` adapter reads the installed eve session API's captured durable tail and reuses the browser event projection. Pages preserve raw-event cursor positions while excluding reasoning, tool payloads, provider errors and runtime metadata. A page is limited to 100 events and 64 KiB of projected events; an event that does not fit is retried from the same cursor, and an oversized first event fails without silently truncating text. A five-second deadline bounds authority checks and provider reads. Cancellation disposes late-opened streams without waiting for provider cleanup. Current authority is checked before runtime access and again before returning the whole buffered page.

Six focused tests cover captured-tail pagination, private-event filtering, byte-boundary replay, oversized/truncated streams, final-check revocation, idle cancellation, late stream disposal and stalled authority checks. Typechecks pass. This internal helper is not yet mounted: agent-to-conversation authorization, protected runtime routing, owned cursors, database concurrency and real-runtime integration remain in task 2.1. No tool or public instruction advertises conversation reads yet.

# CLI private credential storage — 2026-10-09

Added private POSIX storage with exact origin/grant binding, owner/mode checks, no-follow bounded reads, exclusive temporary writes, file/directory fsync and atomic replacement. Per-grant directory locks serialize refresh. The connection is durably disabled before refresh dispatch; failure cannot replay the old refresh token. Successful responses cannot change principal/client/grant or expand scope. Logout disables use before revocation, retains disabled state after an uncertain reply and deletes the file after confirmed success. Locks are never guessed stale or stolen; recovery from a process crash uses a new login connection.

Six storage tests cover private files, origin/grant isolation, refusal to overwrite, concurrent single refresh, lost replies, principal/scope rejection, failed/retried logout, corrupt/symlink/world-readable files and lock timeout. Two separate Node processes sharing the directory issue exactly one refresh. Final `npm run check` passes 275 application/provider tests, four SMTP tests, typechecks and documentation checks; strict OpenSpec validation passes. Provider HTTP callbacks are controlled fixtures; real browser login, token verification, refresh exchange and runnable CLI remain in task 3.2. No deployed route imports this module, so this increment requires no production deployment.

# CLI OAuth protocol — 2026-10-09

The internal OAuth client validates fixed-origin discovery/resource metadata, registers an exact loopback callback and produces random state/S256 authorization. Callback validation rejects foreign host/path, duplicate fields, wrong state/issuer and extra parameters before exchange. Valid attempts become consumed before network dispatch, so denial and lost replies cannot replay a code. Public-only ES256 JWKS verification enforces issuer/resource/client, actor kind, requested scopes, expiry and requester subject binding. Refresh validates the same grant/principal and rotation; revocation remains bound to the configured resource. HTTP uses manual redirects, fixed endpoints, deadlines and bounded JSON without printing raw errors.

Four protocol tests exercise successful exchange/refresh/revocation and the negative cases with real signed JWTs and controlled HTTP responses. Final `npm run check` passes 279 application/provider tests, four SMTP tests, typechecks and documentation checks; strict OpenSpec validation passes. The browser listener, credential-store integration, runnable commands and real production CLI journey remain open; task 3.2 is not complete. This internal module changes no production routes.

# Live Google host-login blocker and probe — 2026-10-09

The actual `/app` Continue with Google action reached Google and failed before account selection with `Error 400: redirect_uri_mismatch`. Google's error details identify the exact submitted callback as `https://mriseqztcwmezvtawnbo.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`. The existing Google Cloud Console browser session is signed out and has reached an account password challenge; operator sign-in was requested without asking for credentials in chat. No OAuth client setting was changed, and no live host login or Calendar consent is claimed.

Added `npm run probe:google-login -- --origin <https-origin> --project <project-ref>` to reproduce the initial redirect boundary without login credentials. It validates the selected project, application return and Google callback, fences redirect destinations, bounds the start body/redirect count/deadline and prints only sanitized diagnostic codes. It stops at Google's error redirect rather than downloading the provider error page. A Google account-selection page can pass only the initial redirect check; the result always states `liveLoginVerified: false`.

## Conversation admission budgets — 2026-10-09

The `bound-conversation-usage` change enforces shared fixed-window admission budgets: 20/minute and 100/hour per host account or requester request, and 200/minute and 2,000/hour service-wide. Private reusable counter rows are locked service-first after existing authority locks. A second current-authority check after quota waits protects elapsed grants/sessions/requests. Charging and accepting new input commit atomically; exact accepted retries, invalid/busy input, reads, settlement and delivery do not charge. Audience switches, host sessions and requester recovery cannot reset a principal's allowance.

Photon and AgentMail defer throttled jobs for one minute without completing their receipt or admitting model input. Earlier receipts retain their order. Email preserves prepared content and refunds only the quota-deferred claim attempt; an expired worker lease cannot alter its job or refund its attempt. Deferred execution still rejects revoked authority. Web returns a typed 429, retains the original message and retry key, and displays specific wait guidance while structured controls remain available. Unknown/network errors retain the existing uncertain-send recovery text.

- Clean rebuild of all 93 local migrations; 1,500 SQL assertions across 32 files pass. The isolated 27-assertion quota test also passes with the requester allowance cleared for the service-hour ceiling, so that check cannot pass through a principal denial. New SQL coverage proves privacy, atomic charging, invalid/busy/replay exemptions, host/request isolation, replacement credentials, independent minute/hour rollover and service ceilings.
- Real concurrent acceptance across 24 host scopes admits exactly 20. Eight request principals with only two service slots admit exactly two. Observed lock waits prove wall-clock window rollover and expired-grant denial with unchanged counters and no runtime input.
- Photon and requester-email integrations pass quota delay, recovery and revocation checks. Ordered email receipts cannot overtake a throttled predecessor; repeated deferral preserves failure attempts. An observed quota lock wait beyond the email lease leaves the expired lease/job unchanged and returns lease loss.
- The initial full integration run passed 33 existing tests but exposed a new fixture's duplicate request token and cleanup error. The fixture now uses unique tokens and independent guaranteed cleanup. All three affected integrations pass on the final migration (13 seconds). No runtime failure was hidden or reclassified as a pass.
- The first new browser assertion exposed the chat hook's generic error masking the known 429. The hook now distinguishes only the known temporary quota error and keeps raw/unknown errors private. Both final browser suites pass (67 seconds), including actual database throttling, retained input, same-message retry, lost-acknowledgment recovery and all retained setup/scheduling journeys.
- Final `npm run check` passes 299 application/provider tests, eight script tests, types and documentation checks; both production service builds pass. Strict OpenSpec validation passes. The rollback-only production probe passes locally and retains zero probe invitations. Its initial local-only draft used a reserved PL/pgSQL variable name; this was corrected before production use.

## Bounded model invocation foundation — 2026-10-09

The `bound-model-execution` change defines conversation/ranking invocation limits and durable per-work/principal/service reservations. Its first implementation step adds the shared model wrapper, not yet wired into production. The wrapper rejects oversized UTF-8 input (including tool schemas and response format), attachments and provider-executed tools before reserving; clamps output to 4,096 or a lower caller cap; and forces stateless, standard-tier low-reasoning calls. No production path may use a process-local substitute for durable reservations.

## Durable model allowance foundation — 2026-10-09

Added private fixed-window principal/service allowance rows and permanent per-work attempt counters. The service-only conversation reservation requires matching current grant, pending accepted message and canonical runtime session. Ranking reservation reuses current authority/evidence locks and checks the manifest, nonempty candidates and absence of a saved result. Each actual attempt reserves 60 cents against 3,000-cent principal and 30,000-cent service limits; work ceilings are eight conversation attempts or two ranking attempts. The reservation policy intentionally overestimates token cost and is not invoice accounting. Authority and time-based evidence are rechecked after budget waits, and all failed reservations roll back together.

# Photon adapter boundary acceptance — 2026-10-09

Task 4.3 is complete against installed eve 0.71.3 and Photon SDK 2.2.0. The native-adapter tests execute the bundled adapter with synthetic downstream/network boundaries: valid/forged signatures are distinguished, HTTP 200 returns while application processing is pending, a subsequent downstream failure cannot change that acknowledgment, duplicate deliveries invoke downstream processing again, and repeated `postMessage` calls do not receive the application's saved identity. These are concrete reasons to retain the application-owned receiver/inbox and outgoing intent. They do not justify a separate bridge process.

| Selected boundary | Executable evidence |
|---|---|
| Authentication and durable acknowledgment | `webhook.test.ts` checks original-byte HMAC, receiver/time/route boundaries, private inbound filtering, body/text limits and waits for the inbox commit; `photon-ingress.test.ts` covers eight concurrent duplicates, lost commit acknowledgments and receiver revocation during a lock wait. |
| Bounded input | The receiver now enforces a five-second body deadline, malformed-length/invalid-UTF-8 rejection and caller cancellation. A real stalled stream whose cancellation promise never resolves still returns 408; oversized and aborted streams also terminate without reaching persistence. |
| Sender-to-host/session ownership | The real signed/eve journey admits and independently links two hosts, retains the same session across web/private continuation, denies cross-host transcript/stream/write/grant substitution and routes replies only to the verified recipient. |
| Ordering and deduplication | SQL covers FIFO after settlement, busy-host fairness, immutable receipt identity and terminal queue acknowledgment. Runtime integration covers lost acknowledgments, restart without repeated model execution, one draft mutation and unlink suppression. |
| Actual SDK send boundary | `photon-grpc.test.ts` starts a loopback gRPC server from the installed SDK's shipped protobufs. It observes the exact client message ID, route, content, authorization and disabled preview/data-detection flags. An unavailable gRPC result causes one send and an uncertain outcome. Reconciliation reads the exact reference and never sends. Every created client closes. Production TLS/retry options are asserted before the test overrides only address/TLS for loopback. |
| Routing and recovery | Transport tests reject ambiguous or changed dedicated lines, preserve the frozen private recipient, reauthorize after preflight, distinguish acceptance/delivery and reconcile uncertainty without a new ID. Linking/handoff and deadline-after-lock integrations preserve separate browser/phone authority. |

The test suite pins `@grpc/proto-loader` 0.8.1 as a direct development dependency so protocol tests do not rely on an accidental transitive import. The initial loopback fixture omitted required message content; correcting that fixture to the shipped protobuf response contract made the actual SDK projection pass. No SDK behavior was mocked for serialization or retries.

The fixture creates a selected requester Calendar connection before proposal evaluation when required, and uses complete physical-neighbor fixtures plus valid initial route estimates before approving in-person meetings. It then runs the actual booking worker, persisted evaluator and dispatch boundary with controlled provider responses.

| Final prerequisite | Verified cases and outcome |
| --- | --- |
| Availability | Newly busy host or requester interval blocks the saved attempt; the requester case proves its selected Calendar is read with its own credential. |
| Grants | Revoked host or requester connection blocks creation. Host revocation during a busy read also prevents dispatch. |
| Decisions and rules | Changed host rules or removed approval blocks creation. A rule change during concurrent route estimates cannot save usable stale evidence. |
| Destination | Changed selected destination, absent catalog entry and read-only access each block creation without fallback. |
| Travel | Fresh inbound/outbound estimates that exceed the gaps, or return no route, block an otherwise approved in-person meeting. Both legs are read. |
| Positive controls | Valid requester Calendar availability and valid in-person travel each produce one confirmed event and two participant outbox records. |

All 13 negative cases verify no provider insert, no dispatch receipt, a blocked immutable attempt, no remaining host reservation and no confirmation outbox. A catalog failure commits the blocked attempt inside evaluation, after which the worker returns `complete` to acknowledge the job; this is distinct from a confirmed booking. The two positive cases demonstrate the same path permits valid work. Existing dispatch tests additionally reject changed agreement, stale or cross-lease evidence, changed rules and revoked request authority after reads.

Validation: the full booking integration passed in 57.9 seconds; all 1,532 SQL assertions in 33 files passed afterward without a reset. `npm run check` passed 316 application/provider tests, eight script tests, TypeScript, lint and documentation links. Test cleanup removes requester grants with their scoped requests. This completes booking task 2.1. No application or schema change was required; production remains on the verified 96-migration state from the preceding lock-order deployment. These are local provider fixtures, not live Google booking acceptance.


## Consent-based booking recovery — 2026-10-09

Extended the existing operator/worker lost-response integration through the actual authenticated `CalendarConsent` and `CalendarSelection` services. The host disconnects after a possible successful write. Reconciliation without a grant, denied consent, and consent to a different Google subject all preserve uncertainty, the exact saved payload, one human approval and the host reservation, without a provider lookup or confirmation. Strict recovery inputs reject added approval evidence and a fabricated confirm action.

The original Google subject is then restored through a bound consent callback using controlled provider tokens. The host explicitly selects a different calendar for future bookings. Reconciliation still GETs the originally dispatched calendar/event; immediate not-found retains the reservation and a later exact matching event completes the original attempt, releases the reservation and publishes two participant confirmations. There is one insert total and no new approval. The adjacent definitive-rejection/retry case preserves event identity, rechecks current decisions and rejects recovery after agreement changes. This completes booking task 2.4; actual live Google consent/booking remains task 3.4.

The SQL suite exposed a local fixture-cleanup race with the minute recovery scheduler. Cleanup now locks its jobs and removes queue publications and jobs in one transaction, so `SKIP LOCKED` recovery cannot republish into the deletion gap. After a fresh 96-migration local rebuild, the booking integration passed (57.1 seconds) and all 1,532 SQL assertions in 33 files passed without another reset; no orphan queue/publication records remained. TypeScript, the two recovery adapter tests, documentation links and strict OpenSpec validation also passed. Only tests and explanatory documentation changed; the deployed application and database definitions are unchanged.


## Booking schema acceptance audit — 2026-10-09

Completed booking task 1.3 against the current desired SQL, reviewed migration history and a fresh full 96-migration local rebuild. `01_foundation.sql` owns durable jobs, transactional queue publication and outbox storage; `04_booking.sql` owns identities, immutable snapshots and reservations; `36_booking_approval.sql` through `41_booking_delivery.sql` add attributable decisions, lease-bound checks/dispatch, atomic worker completion and audience-isolated delivery. The latest reviewed migration is `20261008231233_booking_host_lock_order.sql`; no new schema delta was needed for this audit.

Current local/production catalog queries match for all 11 relevant tables: host approvals, web decisions, booking identities, attempts, reservations, checks, dispatches, deliveries, jobs, queue publications and outbox. Every table has RLS enabled and denies direct SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE to anonymous, authenticated and service roles. Six public booking RPCs—approval, evaluation, dispatch, worker, receipt and delivery—have identical local/production definition hashes and service-only EXECUTE grants. Authorization inside these RPCs is exercised by the booking/SQL integration cases; service access alone is not participant approval.

The fresh booking integration and 1,532 SQL assertions verify failed approvals leave no partial attempts/jobs, duplicate approvals publish one identity/job, uncertainty and not-found retain attempts/reservations, expired or superseded owners cannot complete, and provider-evidenced completion records booked state plus separate confirmation work. Foundation rollback tests verify queue publication belongs to the same database transaction. The reconnect recovery and lost-completion-response cases preserve the existing identity and confirmations. Fixture cleanup leaves no orphan queue or publication records.

Production was inspected read-only for this acceptance audit and remains on the verified 96-migration release state. No runtime or schema changes were required. Process-termination fault coverage and controlled live Calendar acceptance remain the two open tasks in this booking change.


## Booking worker process crash matrix — 2026-10-09

Added a disposable-local-only child-process harness around the actual `BookingWorker.run()` path, real Supabase RPCs and a parent-owned loopback Calendar HTTP fixture. The provider fixture survives worker death. Each selected checkpoint is reported over IPC immediately before SIGKILL; a fresh child must claim and recover durable work. The 23 cases cover pre/post claim, loaded state, evaluation start and credential refresh, persisted destination/success/evidence, worker credential access, dispatch, successful provider insertion, reconciliation lookup, outcome persistence, duplicate terminal acknowledgment and a missing queue wake-up. Reconciliation cases first kill a worker after insertion, then kill its successor before or after lookup.

Assertions verify unchanged frozen payload, one attempt identity, at most one provider insert/event, reservation retention during uncertainty, and zero versus two atomic confirmation records. Death after dispatch but before insertion produces no provider event and remains uncertain with its reservation; recovery does not assume a missing event permits another insert. Terminal redelivery acknowledges existing success without contacting the provider again. Only the dead worker's lease deadline is advanced to simulate recovery time; approval, booking phase, reservation and provider outcomes are not forced.

The expanded booking integration passes in 75.2 seconds. Application checks pass, including TypeScript, eight script tests and 316 app/provider tests. The SQL suite passes all 1,532 assertions in 33 files after both the initial 19-case run and the final 23-case run, without an intervening reset. Documentation checks cover 493 local links; all 20 strict OpenSpec validations pass. This increment completes the process-crash matrix item in the implementation plan. Task 3.3 stays open for the remaining failure-branch termination audit, and task 3.4 still requires controlled live Google booking. No application/schema behavior changed, so no production deployment is needed for this test/documentation commit.


## Booking failure-boundary acceptance — 2026-10-09

Expanded the worker-process matrix from 23 to 43 cases. Six additional normal-path checkpoints cover both sides of load, authority checks and destination/evaluation/evidence persistence. Fourteen failure/recovery cases terminate before and after catalog failure, busy-evaluation outcome persistence, transient-access retry, definitive provider rejection, unavailable provider response, conflicting event evidence and reconciliation credential refresh. Every case uses its own synthetic host, preserving unresolved reservations without exhausting another scenario's admission/model budget. All children execute the real worker with real local RPCs; only provider responses are controlled.

The critical rejection-before-record case leaves a dispatched attempt and no event. Its successor performs lookup only, retains uncertainty/reservation, and creates no replacement. Persisted rejection is terminal and acknowledged without a provider call. Unavailable responses remain uncertain; conflicting provider content remains conflict, with no confirmations. Pre-dispatch busy/catalog outcomes block without an insert when their failure is persisted. A transient retry recovers through fresh evaluation, and interrupted reconciliation refresh still confirms the same previously inserted event. Frozen payload, one attempt, provider insert/event counts, reservation ownership and confirmation counts are checked after each recovery.

The full booking integration passes in 93.8 seconds and also executes duplicate approval/dispatch/queue/delivery cases, both real withdrawal/dispatch lock races, lease expiry, lost RPC responses and overlapping approved reservations. Application checks pass types, eight script tests and 316 app/provider tests. After the integration fixtures are cleaned, all 1,532 SQL assertions in 33 files pass without resetting the database. The 494-link documentation check and all 20 strict OpenSpec validations pass; refreshed apply instructions report 12/13 booking tasks complete. This establishes task 3.3's fault-injection acceptance. Controlled live Calendar booking and lost-response acceptance remains explicitly open in task 3.4. This is a test/documentation change, so the already deployed application and 96-migration database require no redeploy.

## Private intake browser handoff — 2026-10-09

Completed intake task 2.3. The original consenting browser can inspect pending/bound state and install the same request proof through a separate protected handoff after the authorization-code attempt expires. The request cookie is HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure on HTTPS and expires at the original absolute credential deadline. A claim or retry does not extend authority, rotate a proof, agree to a meeting or approve booking.

Revocation blocks claims/refresh, while existing browser access follows minimal closed receipts and credential expiry. Fixtures are cleaned up.

No native browser zoom, iPhone or named-client acceptance is claimed.
